  quod lac virginis appellatur.  Oportet ergo ut de Terra exaltetur filius virginis et quinta substantia 
  alba post resurrectionem elevetur versus caelos & in fundo vasis & aquae 
  remaneant grossum et spissum. Vase dehinc infrigidato reperies in fundo ipsius 
  faeces nigras arsas combustas separatas a spiritu et quintessentia alba quas  projice. In his temporibus argentum vivum pluit ex aere nostro supra terram 
  novam, quod vocatur argentum vivum ex aere sublimatum, ex quo fit aqua 
  viscosa, munda, alba, quae est vera tinctura separata ab omni fece nigra. Et sic 
  aes nostrum regitur cum aqua nostra purificatur & cum albo colore decoratur 
  Quae dealbatio non fit nisi decoctione & aquae coagulatione. Decoque 
  ergo continuo, ablue nigredinem a latone non manu sed lapide sive igne sive aqua 
  mercuriali nostra secunda, quae est vera tinctura. Sic natura  circulariter ad perfectionem operando seipsam dissolvit copulat sublimat elevat 
  & albescit seperatis fecibus. Sic magisterium nostrum consistit in vapore & aquae 
  sublimatione & inspiratur (ut reliqua omnia) ab aere i.e vapore. Sic nisi aes nostrum 
  per gradus ignis elevetur & libere ascendit ut spiritus aut ut argentum vivum scandens 
  vel ut anima alba a corpore separata & in spiritum sublimatione delata, nihil fit. 
  sic in tali regimine corpus fit spiritus & spiritus incorporatur & unitur cum corpore & in 
  tali conjunctione sublimatione & elevatione omnia fiunt alba. Sic pax componitur 
  inter corpus et spiritum quod fiere nequit nisi separentur in has partes. Quare 
  oportet utrumque sublimare ut purum ascendat & impurum & terrenosum descendat 
  cendat in turbatione maris procellosi. Quare decoque continuo quousque corpus assumat 
  & attrahat animam albam mercurialem sibi comparem in propinquitate  naturae primae purae & simplicis. Sic decoque ut nihil de pinguidine animae remaneat 
  quod non fuerit elevatum & sic utrumque erit reductum ad aequalitatem simplicem 
  & simplicem albedinem. Vultur ergo volans per aerem & bufo gradiens per terram est 
  magisterium. Mater (inquit corpus) me genuit & per me gignitur ipsa & postquam ab ea 
 accepi  volatum ipsa meliori modo quo potest nutrit & fovit filium ad statum perfectionis. 
  Postquam corpus cum anima alba ascendit & terra residua descendit videbis aquam coagulare 
  seipsam cum suo corpore in siccum, sicut coagulum agni, lac coagulat in caseum 
  & sic spiritus penetrat corpus & miscentur per minima & corpus attrahit sibi   humorem suum sive animam albam sicut Magnes ferrum. Et haec est sublimatio & 
  coagulatio nostra omne volatile retinens quae facit fugam perire. Et haec est frigidi 
<2.4v> Page Image8  cum calido et humidi cum sicco connectio & mixtio, et conversio naturarum  contrariarum spiritus in corpus & corpus in spiritum sic etiam mixta & in unum  redacta se invicem vertunt, corpus incorporat spiritum & spiritus mutat corpus in spiritum 
  tinctum & album, & fiunt unum permanens & haec est solutio corporis &  coagulatio spiritus quae unam et eandem habet operationem. Qui ergo noverit 
  ducere praegnantem facere, mortificare putrefacere generare species vivificare 
  lumen album inducere & mundare vulturem a tenebris & nigredine quousque 
  igne purgetur & coloretur & a maculis ultimis purificetur a Regibus venerandus est. 
 Quare maneat corpus in aqua donec solvatur in pulverem novum in 
  fundo vasis & aquae & haec est corruptio corporis quae vocatur a sapientibus 
  Saturnus, Aes, Plumbum Philosophorum, & pulvis discontinuatus, & hic sunt tria 
  signa color niger discontinuatio partium & odor faetidus sicut sepulchrorum. In quo 
  cinere (de quo Philosophi tanta dixerunt) est Diadema Regis & Argentum 
  vivum nigrum immundum a quo nigredine purgatur decoquendo continuo in 
  aqua nostra donec elevetur sursum in album colorem qui vocatur anser & 
  Pullus Hermonis. Quia qui terram rubeam denigrat & albam reddit habet 
  magisterium, ut etiam ille qui occidit vivum & resuscitat mortuum. Dealba 
  ergo nigrum & rubefac album ut perficias opus, & cum videris albedinem 
  apparere veram quae splendet sicut gladius denudatus scias quod rubor in ista 
  albedine est occultus, et tunc non oportet illam albedinem extrahere sed  coquere tantum ut cum siccitate & caliditate superveniat citrinitas & rubedo 
  fulgentissima 
 <The remainder of this page is blank except for possible shelfmarks and indications of library ownership.>   ^Note1  An ellipsis in the text occurs here. The text as given in  Trois traitez de la philosophie naturelle  (1612) includes the following missing phrase "humidum terminatum, id est aurum corpus nostrum, per reiterationem liquefactionis in aqua nostra dissolutiva, convertitur & reducitur in sulphur & argentum vivum fixum, & sic". Note that missing text occurs between the repeating words "& sic", so the copyist probably skipped from the first to the second, omitting the intervening text. 
[ ^](javascript:void($njq('#ellipsis_1').btOff());)Note2  The beginning of this paragraph is largely a paraphrase of the text as given in  Trois traitez de la philosophie naturelle  (1612), p. 11. The paragraph there begins "Operare", which Newton wrote at the beginning of the paragraph and then deleted. With the emphasis here showing the words Newton retains, the text in  Trois traitez  reads: "Operare ergo cum ea, & consequeris quod desideras ab ea. Nam  est  spiritus &  anima Solis & Lunae , oleum, & aqua dissolutiva,  fons, balneum Mariae , ignis contra naturam,  ignis humidus , ignis secretus, ocultus, & invisibilis, atque acetum acerrimum, de quo quidam antiquus philosophus dicit, Rogavi Dominum, & ostendit mihi unam aquam nitidam, quam cognovi esse purum acetum alterans, penetrans, & digerens. Acetum (inquam) penetrativum, & instrumentum movens ad putrefaciendum, resolvendum, & reducendum aurum vel argentum in sui primam materiam, & est  unicum agens  in toto mundo in hac arte  quod  videlicet  potest resolvere & reincrudare corpora metallica sub conservatione suae speciei . Est igitur solum medium aptum & naturale per quod debemus resolvere corpora perfecta Solis & Lunae mirabili & solemni solutione sub conservatione suae speciei, & absque ulla destructione, nisi ad novam, nobiliorem, & meliorem formam, sive generationem, scilicet in lapidem perfectum philosophorum, quod est secretum & arcanum eorum mirabile." 
[ ^](javascript:void($njq('#paraphrase_1').btOff());)Note3  This paragraph is largely a paraphrase of the text as given in  Trois traitez de la philosophie naturelle  (1612), p. 13, which—with the emphasis here showing the words Newton retains—reads: "Ergo natura emendatur in sua consimili natura, id est, aurum & argentum, in nostra aqua emendantur, &  aqua  etiam cum ipsis corporibus, quae etiam  dicitur medium animae , sine quo nihil agere possumus in arte ista, & est  ignis vegetabilis, animalis, & mineralis conservativus spiritus fixi Solis & Lunae, destructor corporum ac victor : quia destruit, diruit atque mutat corpora & formas  metallicas, facit que illas  non esse corpora, sed spiritum fixum, illasque convertit in substantiam humidam, mollem & fluidam, habentem ingressum & virtutem intrandi in alia corpora imperfecta &  misceri cum eis per minima, & illa tingere & perficere, quodquidem non poterant cum essent corpora  metallica sicca & dura , quae non habent ingressum, neque virtutem  tingendi & perficiendi  imperfecta.  Benè igitur corpora convertimus in substantiam fluidam , quia unaquaeque tinctura plus in millesima parte tingit in liquida substantia & molli, quam in sicca ut patet de croco . Ergo transmutatio metallorum imperfectorum, est impossibilis fieri per corpora perfecta sicca, nisi prius reducantur in primam materiam mollem & fluidam. Ex his oportet, quod revertatur humidum, & reveletur absconditum. Et hoc est, reincrudare corpora, id est, decoquere & mollire donec priventur coporalite dura & sicca: quia siccum, non ingreditur nec tingit, nisi tingatur, quia (ut dictum) spissum terreum non ingreditur nec tingit, quia non intrat, ergo non alterat; non idcirco tingit aurum, donec  spiritus eius occultus extrahatur à ventre eius per aquam nostram  albam,  & fiat  omnino spiritualis, &  albus fumus, albus spiritus, & anima mirabilis ."[ ^](javascript:void($njq('#paraphrase_2').btOff());)Note4.5  The text as given in  Trois traitez de la philosophie naturelle  (1612) uses the verb "sumatur" here, which Newton apparently also wrote at first, splitting "su" and "matur" at the end of l. 1. He then struck through the "su" on l. 1, added a "p" at the beginnning of l. 2, struck out the "m" and wrote "on" above to make "ponatur."<3.1r> Page Image3   1    The Hyeroglyphicall figures of  Nicholas Flammel  explained, anno 1399.    I Nicholas Flammel bought of a souldier for 2 florens a booke, the cover of it was of brasse,   well bound, all engraven with strange letters neither latin nor Gaule; the leaves of   barks of trees, (with latin letters first ingraven in with a point of iron & then coloured,) were three times   seaven (being soe numbered on the top) & every seventh leafe was without any   writing, but instead thereof upon the first seventh leafe there was painted a  <The following librarian marking runs vertically along the left margin.>   Mo. Var. 259 3 
  Virgin & Serpents swallowing her up; In the second seventh, a Cross where   a serpent was crucifyed; & in the last seventh there painted desarts   or wildernesses, in the midst whereof ran many faire fountaines, from   whence there issued out a number of serpents which ran up & down   here & there.   On the first leafe was written in Capitall letters: [ Abraham the Jew , Prince,   Preist, Levite, etc: to the nation of the Jews etc.] And curses were added to any   that should read it but a Sacrificer or a Scribe.   In the second leafe he exhorted his nation against vice, & to expect the Messias.   In the third & following leaves to helpe his captive nation to pay   tribute to the Roman Emperors & to doe other things, he taught them the  transmutation of Metalls; hee painted the vessells by the sides, & advertised them of the   colours, & of all the rest, save the first agent, which he spake not a word of but 
  only painted it entire in the fourth & fift leaves, which yet noe man could have   understood (though well painted) without being well skilled in their traditionall cabala   & without having well studyed their books. On the said fourth & fifth leaves was nothing   written but only these 4 figures painted on their foure sides, viz:   1. A young man with wings at his ankles, having in his hand a Caducean Rod   writhen about with two serpents wherewith hee stroke upon a helmet which covered   his head, hee seemed to my small judgment to bee the God  Mercury  of the Pagans:   against him there came running & flying with open wings a great old man who   upon his head had an howerglasse fastened & in his hands a hook or Sith like death   with the which in terrible & furious manner hee would have cut of the feet of  Mercury .   2 There was a faire flower on the top of a very high Mountain which was   sore shaken with the north wind; it had the foot blew, the flowers white & red,   the leaves shining like fine gold: And round about it the Dragons & Griffins of the   north made their nests & abode [Ascende in montem ut vegetabilem saturniam  regalem et mineralem herbam videas. succus ejus sumatur purus abjectis faecibus Venus est hic ☿  Philos. Flam. c trac   3. A farre rose tree flowered in the midst of a sweet garden climing up   against an hollow oake at the foot whereof boyled a fountaine of most white   water which ran headlong down into the depths notwithstanding it first passed the   hands of infinite people which digged in the earth seeking for it; but because they   were blind none of them knew it, except here & there one which considered the   weight.   4 A King with a great fauchion, who made to bee killed in his presence by   some souldiers a great multitude of little infants whose mothers wept at the   feet of the unpityfull souldiers: the blood of which infants was afterwards by   other souldiers gathered up & put in a great vessell wherein the sun & moon   came to bath themselves.   These 4 pictures I caused to be painted that I might shew them to get the  interpretation. And tryed many ways for 21 yeares in vaine, yet I medled not with blood   (that being wicked) for I found in my book that the Philosophers called blood the   minerall spirit that is in mettalls principally in ☉ , ☽ , & ☿   MS Var 259 | 3  <3.1v> Page Image2    At last in my Pilgramage in  Spaine  I met with one  Canches  a Jew   converted to Christianisme, & Physitian, who overjoyed with the copy of the cuts I shewed to   him from time expounded more & more of them to mee finding great mysterys even to   points & after he expounded the greatest part of them to mee hee dyed at  Orleance  of a   sicknesse gotten by our sea viage.   Soe then I knew the first principles but not their first preparation which is a thing   most difficult, but after 3 years labour I found that too, as I soon knew by the   strong scent & odour thereof, Which found, I could not erre in the rest if I followed my   book according to the letter.   On Munday January 17, at noon anno 1382 I first made projection on ☿ , turning 1/2℔  into   pure silver & afterwards following my book from word to word, I made projection of the   red stone on the like quantity of ☿ (Aprill the 25 following in the same yeare) into ☉  more soft plyable &   better then that of the mine.   Though I had enough when I had done it once, yet for the pleasure of it I did it   thrice & my wife could tend the work as well as I, & no doubt could have done it all   her selfe if shee would have undertaken it.   When I wrote this commentary 1413 (my wife being then dead) wee had founded   14 hospitalls in  Paris , built three new chappells, inriched 7 churches with new revenues,   & done almost as much at  Bulloyne  as here, etc.   In the fourth Arch of the church yard of the innocents etc I have set Hieroglyphick   which represent (more openly) the last resurrection, & judgment, & (more hiddenly) the linall  process of these mysterys, only of the first agent you shall find nothing either in those   figures or in this booke.   Chapter 2. If thou knowest not the first agent thou canst not understand my figures   but knowing that, consider my Figures colours & rowles but cheifly my rowles for they   speak not vulgarly in this work. Then consider why  Saint Paul  is on the right hand of  Saint   Peeter  (contrary to custome), & why  Paul   is clothed in white & yellow, &  Peeter in  yellow   and red; why the man & woman at the feet of these 2 saints praying to God as if at the   day of Judgment, are aparralled in divers colours & not naked or els nothing but   bones like them that rise againe: & why in this day of Jugment they are painted at the   feet of Saints with them in heaven & not more low in earth. Why the two angells which say in   their Rolls Surgite mortui, venite ad juditium domini mei, are clad in orang colour   & out of their place which should have beene on high in heaven with the two other which play   on the instruments, why they have a feild violet & blew, why their rowl which speaks   to the dead ends in the open throat of the red Lyon. And such like things.   This Vessell of earth is called a tripple vessell, having a stage or flower  in the midst on which is a  boule < or > platter  of ashes holding the glasse filld with the scum   of the redd sea & the fat of the mercuriall wind which thou seest painted in form of a penner & inkhorn. This vessel the envious   call an Athanor, sive, dung, Balneo Mariae Furnace, sphaere, the Green Lyon, prison, grave   urinall, phioll, Bolts-head, & most commonly an oven: In the end of my summary or abridgment   of Philosophy (composed 4 years 2 months since, I called it the house & habitation of the   Poulet, & the ashes of the Platter the chaff of the Poulet.   If the heat bee too great the flowers will bee burnt or the Glasse burst, if too little   thou shalt never see an end, the cold natures wanting heat to digest them & if the fire   goe out the work is spoyled. Therefore let thy heat bee according to winter or <3.2r> Page Image3  rather according to the heat of a Bird, which begins to fly so softly from the signe   if aries to that of cancer: for know that the infant at the beginning is full of cold   flegme & milk, & too great heat is an enemy to cold & moisture, & will not suffer   the two elements of cold & heat to embrace each other etc. Therefore favour not one nature   more then another etc. When the golden Bird, saith  Diomedes  shall bee come just to   Cancer & that from thence it shall run towards libra, thou mayst augment the   fire a little & in like manner when this faire bird shall fly from libra towards   Capricorne, which is the desired Autumn, the desired harvest, & of the fruits that arenow ripe   Chap 1. Theologicall interpretations. The two Dragons (sins) of colour black & blew in   a feild sable one with wings (which may be like most sins chased away) guilded (most sins being caused   by covetousness of ☉ ) the other without wings which cannot bee chased away as the sins against   the holy ghost.)   Chap 3. These are the true principles or beginnings of Philosophy, which the sages durst not shew their own children The male & fixed, (our 🜍 ) is under the female , volatile black & obscure (or ☿ ) which   endeavours to get the dominion for many months. These are our principles, the Sunn & ☽ ;   The Serpents & Dragons painted by the Egyptians in a circle the head biting the   tayle, to signify that they proceed from the same root& that it alone was sufficient & that they , are perfected by circulation: the   Dragons which keept golden Apples of the Virgins Hesperides: These are they on whom  Jason  in his adventure for the golden Fleece poured the broth or liquor prepared by the   faire Medea: The two serpents given & sent by  Juno  (i.e. the nature Metallick) which  Hercules  (i.e. the Philosopher) must strangle in his craddle (i.e. overcome, kill, & make   to putrefy corrupt & ingender at the beginning of his work: The 2 serpents twisted   round the Caducean rod of Mercury, with which he exerciseth his great power &   transformeth himself as hee listeth: The Corassene bitch & Armenian dogg (sayeth  Avicenna ) which in the vessel of the sepulcher bite & worry each other continually till both, by their   slavering venom & mortall wounds bee all over of a goare blood, which killing each   other are stewed in their propper venome which after death changeth them into living &   permanent water: The two sperms masculine & feminine (described in the beginning of my   Abridgment of Philosophy) engendered (say  Rasis  &  Avicenna  &  Abraham the Jew ) within   the veines & entrails of the 4 Elements: The radicall moisture of metalls, 🜍  & argent vive:   The two sperms gathered from the dung odure & rottennesse of the Sun & ☽  to make   a triakle to heale all distempers & prolong life.   The reason why I painted them Dragons etc: because in the putrefaction the   exhalations arising in the glasse are black blew & yellowish, & the stinch (which the Philosopher feels not without   breaking his glasse) is able to kill any thing living with its poysonous rancour.   The Pontique fierynesse & sharp vertue of the poyson of our ☿  (i.e. its internall   fire) resolving into a pure cloud, that is, into impalpable pouder, all things that resist it, this   heat works upon the radicall metallik viscous oyly moisture into blacknesse. this is the   black sail with which  Theseus  returned from  Crete  which caused his fathers (our ☉ s ) death   that from the ashes of this Phoenix another King may spring.   The colours blewish & yellowish (but not the fatall Orange colour or 1/2 red) precede   the blacknesse, which are perfected in blacknesse in 40 days but the blacknesse is not   destroyed, in lesse then' 5 months, after which immediately follows whitenesse   This dissolution som call Death, destruction, (whence so many alegorys of dead men graves 
  tombs, etc) others Calcination, denudation, seperation, Trituration, & assation becaus the matter   is turned into discontinuous small parts; others Reduction into the first matter, mollification   extraction, commixtion, liquefaction, conversion of Elements, subtiliation, division,  humation, impastation, distillation, because the matter is brought back to seed, softened, &  circulated; others Elixir or Iris, putrefaction, corruption, Cymmerian darknesse, a gulfe, Hell   Dragons, Generation, ingression, submersion, complection, conjunction, Impregnation, because   the black watersh matters are perfectly mingled, & hold of one another <3.2v> Page Image4    first the bodys become a pouder or fat & glutinous water which copiously circulates till   it bee first like a black broth somewhat fat which thickens at last to a black pitch   or earth called Terra faetida stinking like graves; This  Hermes  calls Terra foliata or   the earth of leaves but its propper name is Laton which must afterward bee whitened.   The ancient Sages that were Cabalists have described it in their Metamorphoses under   the History of the serpent of Mars, which had devoured the companions of Cadmus   who slew him peircing him with his lance against an hollow Oake. Note this Oake   Chap: 1. There is next the Dragons a man & woman in Orange (which colour signifys   dispaire or letting goe the hope of things in this life) in a feild azure & blew (to mind   us of heavenly things)   Chap 4. This man & wife may teach that in this second operation the two natures or 4 Elements (☉  dry  hot ), (☽  moist  cold ) are truly   but not yet perfectly conjoyned the crows head being the Androgine or Hermaphrodite of the Ancients. Learn the mediators & peacemaker twixt the Elements, videlicet   the black water is a medium to reconcile heat & cold, being of kin to both, to   hot by its heat, to cold by its moisture, & the black earth (into which this water will  coagulate) mediate twixt moist & dry being akin to moist by its coldnesse & to dry by its  drynesse. Dost thou not consider a most perfect mixture of all the foure Elements   having first turned them into water & now into Earth? I will hereafter teach   the conversions into aire or white 🜍  & into fire or red 🜍 .   Secondly I painted two bodys to signifie that thou must divide (into two parts azoth & laton) what hath beene coagulated, to nourish the   young infant with milk i.e. to wash laton with azoth; hee which is washed is   the serpent Python which (generated from the corrupted slime of the earth when all was water) must   bee killed & conquered by the arrows of  Apollo  our yellow Sun, that is our fire equall to   that of the sun: hee which washeth or rather the washings are the teeth of that serpent   which the Artist  Theseus  will sow in the same earth whence armed Souldiers shall spring   up, & at last discomfit themselves, & bee resolved by opposition into the same nature of earth againe,   leaving the workman conqueror. For the earth dissolvs, congeals, blackens, whitens, kills, &   quickens it selfe.   The blew feild is one of the first colours that the dark woman lets us see. The man   & woman are almost all orange coloured, to signify that the bodys are yet moist & undigested   & soe will bee in part according to these present colours. But these confections   (called Hummus, Ethelia, Arena, Boritis, Corsufle, Cambar, Albar AEris,  Duenech, Randeric Kukul Thabricis, Ebisemech, Ixir etc) as they grow dry will   become white there first appearing a white (or citrine or yellowish, saith Scala   Philos) circle of haire round the matter by the vessel sides signifyed by a white   rowle or circle about the womans body.   The mans rowle saith, man shall come to the judgment of God; that is, This   must come to the colour of perfection to bee judged & clensed from all his blackness   & filth & to bee spiritualized & whitened. The womans rowl saith surly that day   shall bee terrible, i.e. Horror holds them in prison 80 days in the darknesse of the   waters in the extream heat of the Summer & troubles of the sea, before the king can   bee whitened   Chap 1. In a feild sinople (i.e. Green) are two men and a woman rising againe   one out of a Sepulcher the other man & the woman out of the Earth, all three pure   white, lifting their hands towards their eys & their eys up to heaven. over these are two   angells sounding musicall instruments & over these our Saviour, holding the world in his   hand, on whose head an angell sets a crown assisted by two others, which say in their rowls   O pater omnipatens, O  Jesu  bone: On his right side is  Saint Paul  clothed in white & yellow   with a sword at whose feet a man clothed in gowne of orange in which appears black   & white folds, asks pardon of his sins holding his hands together from between which proceed <3.3r> Page Image5  these words Dele mala quae feci. On the other hand is  Saint Peter  with his key clothed   in redish yellowor citrine red, holding his right hand on a woman clad in a gown of orange colour, on   her knees with her hands together having a roule inscribed Christe precor esto pius.   (i.e. bee pitifull): Behind whom is an Angell kneeling with a rowl that saith salve domin <e>    angelorum. There is also another angel kneeling on the otherside behind the man, holding   a rowle that saith O rex sempiterne.   Chap 5. Dele mala quae feci, that is, tolle nigredinem (gladio tuo). Hermes in his book of the Secret art   bids out of the blackmans head & the crows head, &  Lamsprig  that noble German in the  Commentary of his Hieroglyphicks saith in this wood is a black beast whose head being cut of hee becomes   white, that is when the cloud appears noe more this body is said to bee without a head.   The sages bid cut of the head of the viper Derexa, (i.e. take away his blacknesse)   For every head of the serpent Hydra cut of, there springs out   ten in its place: that is, every time our stone is dissolved & whitened or congeled againe it vertue is   multiplyed tenfold.  Saint Paul s sword is the white stone, it hath 5 whole wreaths or rounds to shew that   there must bee five whole imbibitions which must bee done in five whole months, a   month to every imbibition: See here the reason why  Haly Abenragel  said the boyling   of the things is done in thre times 50 days. The two ends of the sword are not  wreathed which represent the beginning & ending of the work, [Only there are two half wreaths   which signify that] you must imbibe at the first time gently, giving it but little milk   as to a new born child least Isir bee drowned, & you must doe the like when our king   is full & will have noe more, & these two imbibitions are but of few days. But in the   5 middle imbitions give him abundantly that the virgins milk compasse all the matter   round about, (for our Salamander livs in the fire & is fire & argent vive that runs in the   fire fearing nothing). Hence one that was envious (reconing the two short & little   imbibitions) said our head of the Crow is leprous, & therefore he that would clens it   must mak it goe down seaven times into the River of Regeneration of Jordan   as Naaman a  did. The wreaths are black to signify the imbibitions or blacknesses, for   the fire with moisture causeth blacknesse.   Virgins milk being dryed upon this body will colour it with white yellow like  Saint   Paul s Garments, in which colour thou must make thy Corsufle to come. And   when thou seest the golden & red Citrine border of  Saint Paul s garment thou hast   obteined mercy, & the whitnesse shall immediately follow & then is the mother (☿  which went   out to make the imbitions & fermentations & to teine & colour the body or infant) put   into the infants belly which shee had brought forth. Then hast thou overcom the  enchanted Bulls that cast fire & smoak out of their nostrills:  Hercules  hath cleansed the stable   of black rottennesse;  Jason  hath powerd the broth or decoction upon the Dragons of   Cholchos; & thou hast in this power the horn of Amalthea to get which thou must   fight valiently like  Hercules ; for this Achelous this moist river is indewed with a   mighty force besides that he often transforms himself from one forme to another;   which transfigurations are described in the book of the 7 seals, viz: in passing from black to   white it puts on all colours imaginable, it often melts & coagulates againe amidst which   divers operations (performed at one & the same time) it grows citrine green red (but not   true red) it will become blew & orange untill overcom by drynesse all these infinite   colours end in this admirable citrine whitenes of the Colour of  Saint Paul s garments.   which in short time will become of the colour of the naked sword; which by longer &   stronger decoction will take in the end a red citrine colour & afterwards the vermilion   red. Note that the milk of the Moon is not as the virgins milk of the sun: for the   imbibitions of whitenes require a whiter milk then those of a golden rednesse. In this   (albification) I had erred but for  Abram the Jewe  for it is a most difficult work, <3.3v> Page Image proceeding contrary to the first work, that dissolving into black water this coagulating into white earth.  6  Chap 6. I have painted three resuscitants in a feild vert or Greene becaus in this   decoction (following the black) the bodys becom green & keep that colour longer then any other after the   black. O happy green (saith the Rosary without which nothing can vegetate.   These are the body soul & spiritof our stone, our sun moon & mercury, are raised from death to life   from blackenesse to whitnesse are made elements of aire & whitened, wherefore I painted them   all white ; the body lifting up its tombstone; the soule, because it cannot bee buried, only   seeking its body amongst the tombs like a woman with her haire dishevelled; & for the   same reason the  spickt  is made like a man coming out of the earth not of a tomb   The body is the black earth which wee make white, the soul is the other half divided   from the body which washes it white, & by its imbibitions & fermentations givs it a vegetable   soule or powr to vegetate & multiply, the spirit is the tinctire & drynesse, which as a   spirit hath power to pierce all metalline things; & these three unite inseperably into   one white confection in which they are as the body soule & spirit are in a man united together.   Now look up to the king ( Jesus ) crowned who having conquered death shall save & unite   eternally to himself all pure soules (metalline natures) & drive away all impurity ( 
 heterogeneous matter) from him. Tis a pure Silver that hath passed the Coppell & is 7 times refined   saith  David .   The two angells below (with the lute & haultboy) & the 3 above him (two with their rowls O pater   omnipotens, O bene  Jesu , & a third crowning him) are divine spirits celebrating the   marveiles of god in this operation.   Chap i. The two angells of orang colour in a blew & violet feild & the man in red vermilon holding the  vermilion red Lyons foot gaping as it were to devour the man are described.   Chap 7. The blew & violet field of the Angells shew the colours coming out of the  mercuriall moisture which thou (imbibing it with virgins milk of the Sun) hast dryed upon the stone   for the stone not abounding so much with moisture as before, shall not have much black but   instead thereof violet blew & the Peacock tayle in his imbitions   I have made two angells with wings to denote the two natures ☿  & 🜍  to be now   all spiritualized, both being fixed (corporeall) together & both flying & circulating together being most subtile spirituall & true tinctures.   Now remember to begin the rubifying by the apposition of mercury citrine   red, but thou must not power on much & only once or twice according as thou   shalt see occasion for this operation ought to bee don by a dry fire & by a dry   sublimation & calcination. And here I tell thee a secret which thou shalt seldom find   written but I must not proceed here too far contenting my self to shew the things   which will teach every (whom God will permit to know) what property the signe of the   balance or libra hath when it is enlightened with the sun & mercury in the   month of october.   The Orang colour of the angells denote the colour when thy white confections   have beene more digested & the black of the violet & blew chased away by the fire   which orang colour is compounded of the expected citrine Red & the remainder of the   violet & blew not yet quite banished.   Also this Orang colour denoteth that the natures are gradually by little & little   digested & perfected. The roule [surgite mortui, venite ad juditium domini mei is   only of a Theologicall meaning, but it ends in the Lyons Throat to signify the continuity   of the work.   Chap 8. A woman in orang colour (the stone almost perfected) prayeth  Saint Peeter  (who   clothed in citrine red is the Mercury of our Sun) to grant her multiplication, & a more   rich acouterment like that he is clothed with: the man grants it by laying his hand on   her shoulder. I have put a woman rather then a man or an angell (though the whole natures now bee both spirituall & corporal masculine & feminine because   these are a womans desires. And I have given the man a key to signify his power <3.4r> Page Image7  to open & shut losse & bind, dissolve & congeale (multiplication being usually denoted   with these expressions [aperi claude; solve, liga]; & every opening & shuting of the   stone (every solution & fixation) multiplys its vertue ten fold, arising from to a 100 thence to   a 1000 etc. Now to shew thee with what mercury thou oughtest to doe this &   when, I have given the man a garment citrine red & the woman one of   Orange colour. But I must not say too much.   Her rowl (Christe precor esto pius) is a prayer that now the work is come soe far   all may not bee spoyled with two much heat which may break the glass though it cannot   hurt the stone. Therefore govern the work sweetly with patience, augment the fire   a little but not two 3414r_1  much.   Chap 9. The violet & dark (or dark violet feild in which the man & Lyon are   denote that the woman (our Stone) by her full decoction hath obtained the faire citrine red   garments she demanded of  Saint Peeter  having left her orang colour. And the vermilion   redness (scarlet in graine, granado red) of the Lyon denotes that all is now   perfect. Shee is now like a Lyon devowring all metallick nature & turning it to pure   gold, a flying lyon which carrys & lifts up this man out of the miserys & snares of   this life the standing waters of Egypt, to mak him depise riches etc. God bee thanked   who hath granted us to see this Tyrian Purple this wild poppy of the rock.  3424r_2  <3.4v> Page Image<This page is blank except for possible shelfmarks and indications of library ownership.>   ^Note1  Newton, or his source, has "two" where the context clearly indicates that "too" should have been used.[ ^](javascript:void($njq('#anno_3-4r_1').btOff());)Note2  Compare to Flamel's hieroglyphs in Pierre Arnauld, Trois traitez de la philosophie naturelle non encore imprimez (Paris, 1612).  Courtesy Roy G. Nevelle Historical Chemical Library, Chemical Heritage Foundation.<4.1r> Page Image4    Novum Lumen Chymicum.  Sendivogii  . Divi Leschi genus amo.   Ms. Var. 259 4   1    ‡ <in mg:>Tractatus 2. ‡   Natura est velle Dei sicut semen est velle Naturae (i.e. Deus fecit Naturam & natura   semen in Elementis. Natura obsequitur Spermati (quod est Elixir cuiusque Rei vel Quintessentia vel   perfectissima rei decoctio & digestio, vel Sulphuris Balsamum id est humidum radicale in Metallis ) 
  Quatuor Elementa suum subtile suum aerem liquidem vel vaporem in centrum Terrae mittunt (ut homo semen in uterum faeminae)   & ea spermata vel semina Archeus naturae miscendo rursùs ejecit foras ex quo Semine vel Spermate   pro varietate vasorum varia producuntur.   Tractatus 4 Quando ex centro Terrae sublimatur vapor iste transit per loca   vel frigida vel calida. Si per calida & pura ubi pinguedo Sulphuris parietibus adhaeret   vapor ille, quem Philosophi mercurium Philosophorum dixerunt, jungitur illi pinguedini quam postea secum   sublimat & tunc fit unctuositas & pinguedo (relicto nomine  vaporis ). Si iam sublimatur in loca   per vaporem antecedentem purgata ubi terra est subtilis pura & humida, tum implet   poros ejus, ei jungitur & fit ☉ : Sin loca sint impura & frigida, fit  . Si vero   talis terra sit pura & mixta Sulphuri fit  Venus  etc: (Idem nempe est semen Saturni & Solis   idem Lunae & martis sed locus Terrae diversus est etc). Vapor iste continuò sublimando  purgat loca subtiliando crudum impurum & purum secum trahens, Sic metalla producuntur   ubi ante 1000 annos nulla fuerunt .   Iste vapor unctuosus (sed minùs pinguis) Hyeme in superficie Terrae congelatus, redeunte vere, miscetur   terrae & aquae & fit Magnesia trahens sibi similem Mercurium aeris: Et dat vitam   & ortum omnibus vegetabilibus.   Tractatus 5 Sin iste vapor secum trahit impuritatem terrae (unde loca metallis  generandis aptiora   fiunt ) usque ad superficiem Terrae ,ab aere congelatur (nam aer crudus   habet ingressum in aerem (i.e. vaporem ab aere puro procreatum) ) & sic fiunt omnia   genera lapidum. Vel si locus sit aquosus generatur argilla figularis. Si iste vapor   sine pinguedine in loca aquae purae salis frigidissima, fiunt Adamantes, (nam vapor iste   solus facile sublimat in loca frigida sed oleum seu pinguedo non nisi magno   calore in loca calida ascendit: levi congelatur frigore sed vapor ad loca debita ascendit   & congelatur in aqua pura in lapides granulatim . ) Colores Lapidum fiunt ratione Sulphuris   si Sulphuris pinguedo congelatur, motu illo perpetuo postea spiritus aquae transeundo digerit   & purificat illud vi salis donec rubei vel albi digesti coloris tot reiteratis distillationibus subtiliatus   elevetur & res imperfectas penetrat & colorat qui color jungitur illi aquae partim congelate   & cum ea fixatur fixatione inseparabili. Congelatur enim omnis aqua calore si   est sine spiritu, & frigore si habet spiritum: Sed qui scit congelare aquam calido &   spiritum cum ea conjungere invenit quid auro multò praetiosius ☞  Efficiat igitur ut separetur spiritus ab aqua, ut putrescat & appareat granum   postea rejectis faecibus reducat spiritum ex alto in aquam & faciat conjungere   simul: illa enim conjunctio generabit ramum dissimilem formâ parentibus.   Tractatus Tertius De materia prima Metallorum. Haec materia est duplex  principalis est humidum aeris caliditate mixtum quam Philosophi mercurium dicunt qui radiis Solis   & lunae gubernatur in mare philosophico. Secunda est terrae calliditas sicca quam   vocarunt 🜍 . Cujus pondus ut explicetur considera quod totum corpus non est semen   sed in quolibet corpore est centrum & locus vel spermatis punctum & semper 8200   pars etiam in grano tritici , & haec scintilla necessaria a suo corpore custoditur   ab omni excessivo calore & frigore. Omnia Metalla habent impuritates, observes itaque   punctum naturae quod non quaerendum est in metallis vulgi quae mortua sunt sed in  nostris    viventibus: Enim metallorum vita est ignis dum adhuc sunt in Mineris suis, & mors est   ignis fusionis scilicet.   Prima Metallorum materia est humiditas quaedam mixta aere callido in forma   aquae pinguis adhaerentis unicuique rei purae vel impurae. Provenit aliquando in 
<4.1v> Page Image2  in lucem indutâ (🜍 e )  veste aliquâ; praesertim in locis ubi non habeat adhaerere alicui ; et   haec materia unica est exceptâ veste ejus vel umbrâ i.e. Sulphure.   Tractatus 4. Frustra nituntur Artistae resolvere metalla in suam materiam primam quae   nihil aliud est quam vapor humidus vel aer liquidus (materia scilicet (vel sperma)   communis omnium corporum) qui stillando per poros terrae condensat in aquam   ex qua nascuntur res omnes.  Bernardus Trevisanus  ita disputat optime sed obscurè quia   non de vapore sed Elementis unde hic vapor oritur loquitur.   Tract.6 Omnis res habens semen multiplicatur in eo, sed non sine naturae   auxilio, nam semen nihil aliud est quam aer congelatus in aliquo corpore, seu ~   vapor humidus qui nullius est usus nisi resolvatur calido vapore.   Semen est triplex Animale quod ex imaginatione cognoscitur.   Vegetabile vulgare est ut videmus in fructibus; Quod nobis ostendit quomodo  natura creat illud ex 4 Elementis. Nam Hyems putrefacit congelando spiritus  vitales in Arboribus quos cùm calor Solis (humiditatis attractivus) resolvit, tum calor   naturae per motum excitatus pellit ad circumferentiam vaporem aquae subtilem   qui poros arboris aperiendo, & guttas stillare facit, semper purum ab impuro  separando impurum abit in folia & corticem purum in flores & fructum  congelantur ; Veluti pomum in quo sperma est ex quo nascitur arbor, sed in  spermate est semen vel granum intrinsecè ex quo etiam nascitur arbor nam  multiplicatio non est in spermate sed in semine.   Tertio Minerale Semen quod non creditur quia invisibile nec mirum cùm quod   prae oculis est non percipiunt ignari. Tamen quod superius est est sicut id quod inferius   est & quod inferius (generatur ex semine) sicut id quod superius est. Nihil nascitur &   crescit sine semine sed mortuum est. Metalla sic generantur: Elementa 4 stillant   per Archeum Naturae vaporem aquae ponderosum in centrum Terrae qui est semen   metallorum & dicitur mercurius propter ejus fluxibilitatem & uniuscujuscunque rei  conjunctionem, non propter essentiam, assimulatur Sulphuri propter internum calorem, 
  & post congelationem dicitur humidum radicale. Hic non est mercurius Vulgi qui   corpus est & suum semen habet pro mole corporis exiguum in quo  multiplicatur Sed semen vapor aquae congelatus accipiatur, (quale etiam est semen  animalium). Sic Chemici male corrodunt solvunt sublimant Corpora etc quasi homo   dissecandus sit ut hominem generet, nec ex homine sed ex spermate vel semine   generatur homo, nec ex metallis sed eorum spermatibus fit metallum. Nec homo nec metallum   dissecabitur in sperma 4111v_1 . Conjunge itaque marem & feminam ut inter se  imaginentur sperma & natura operante generabitur fructus. Artifex nihil facit   nisi ut sciat subtile a spisso separare & in vas imponere debitum   Prout res incipit ita finitur: ex uno fiunt duo, ex duobus unum, & non ulteriùs.   Unus est deus hic genuit filium; unus dedit duo, duo unum dederunt spiritum   sanctum ab utroque procedentem: sic factus est mundus & sic erit finis ejus. In his   4 punctis habes Patrem, Patrem & filium, & spiritus sanctus: habes 4 luminaria duo   celestia duo Terrestria; item 4 Elementa. Quicquid est erit fuit in hâc ~   figura patet. Solus Deus creat unum ex uno sufficit si ex 2 tu possis 1   creare. Materia prima non videtur, occulta est in natura vel in Elementis <4.2r> Page Image3  & nemo nisi Deus potest hanc facere sed ex materia secunda   (i.e. Spermate multiplicativo)quae Philosophis apparet in locum debitum positâ natura producet fructus.   Sic terram materiam primam hominis & sperma materiam secundam solus deus creat   & nemo potest facere hominem ex terra sed ex spermate in debito loco posito   producetur homo.   Tractatus 7. Regnum Minerale & Vegetabile sunt independentia & ex uno per   unum creata sed Animale ex utroque fit & dependet & est nobilius iis quia semper in   tertio virtus finitur & in secundo multiplicatur. Enim Res multiplicantur in  materia secunda seminibus non in primâ, neque tu possis primam (ut hominem Arbrem   Aurum etc) creare, sola natura facit & licet habes materiam primam (secundum   Philosophos illud sal centrale) tamen sine auro non possis metallum multiplicare.   In vegetabilibus semen est extra & Renes digestionis est aer calidus, In Animalibus   semen est extra & intra & digeritur in Renibus viri : in Mineralibus aqua sive semen est   in centro cordis & vita & Renes digestionis sunt ignis 4212r_1 . Horum 3 seminum receptaculum   est terra matrix faeminae & Aer. Ea sunt Seminum Receptacula quae 
  sunt congelationes corporum, ea est digestio quae solutio, ea putrefactio quae  destructio . Semen se conjungit unicuique rei in suo Regno, quia subtile est, &   nihil aliud quam aer qui pinguedine in aqua congelatur. Pori corporum   aperiendo sunt emittatur sperma in cujus centro semen iacet quod aer   est; illud quando venit in Matricem debitam congelatur & congelat   quod reperit purum vel impurum puro mixtum.   Corpora debilitantur emissione Seminis ( ut in venereis, & arboribus   nimium fructiferis patet) & moriuntur quando totum consumitur sed non priùs.   Sic Semen est invisibilis sed sperma est visibile, & fere est anima vivens,   non invenitur in rebus mortuis: Elicitur vel suaviter vel cum violentia.   Tractatus 8. Seminis matrix est vita quae facit putrescere sperma vel granum,   & puri puncti congelationem causatur, & calore corporis sui nutrit &   crescere facit per menses annos & tempora etc   Semen in centrum Terrae quasi in renes per Elementa projectum & in   superficiem rejectum , ibi nequit aeque perfici natura ac arte propter aerem   crudum qui penetrat & implet omnes cavitates vel matrices in superficie Terrae.   Tractatus 9. Vir et mulier non differunt ratione seminis vel matricis sed sola   digestione ut matrix purior est in sanguine & sale. Sic luna & Sol non differunt   semine & matrice sed matrix magis habuit de aqua quam de sanguine   digesto secundum tempus lunae caelestis. Ut vero cognoscas quomodo metalla   coeant emittant & recipiant semen consule planetarum ordinem   ♃  ♂  ☉  ♀  ☿  ☽ . Earum virtutes descendunt ideo ex ♂  fit ♀ , ex ♃  ☿ , & et ☽  ex   sed ♀  ☿  vel ☽  non mutantur in ♂  ♃  vel  . Sol miscet se omnibus   sed nunquam per inferiores (♀ , ☿ , ☽ ) melioratur. Si in praedictis mutationibus Solis administrare naturam Chemici scirent thesaurum   invenirent. Metalla itaque sunt invicem conjungenda quorum natura naturae   correspondea 4222r_2 . Propterea unum datur Metallum quod habet potentiam alia   ‡ <in mg:>☞  ‡  consumendi, enim est fere ut aqua eorum & fere mater: Unica tantùm res,   humidum radicale solis & lunae, resistit ei & melioratur per illud: sed   ut detegam Chalybs vocatur. Si undecies coit aurum cum eo emittit suum   semen & debilitatur fere ad mortem usque, concipit Chalybs & generat filium   patre clariorem, postea cum semen iam nati imponitur in suam matricem purgat   illam & facit millesies aptiorem ad pariendum optimos fructus. Est et alius <4.2v> Page Image4  Chalybs qui assimulatur huic , per se a natura creatus, qui scit ex radiis   solis elicere illud quod tot homines quaesierunt & operis nostri principium est   Tractatus 10. Lapis seu Tinctura Philosophorum est aurum in supremum gradum   digestum. Sicut herba est sine semine sed quando maturescit producit semen, sic   aurum Vulgi est sine semine sed quando maturescit dat semen seu tincturam   sed ob aerem crudum & inopiam caloris Natura non faciat aurum ita  maturescere at artifex peritus potest naturam promovere & efficere ut aurum dabit   fructum & semen in quo se multiplicat. Sicut videmus vites in locis frigidis   incipiunt crescere sed non maturescunt ad fructus & semen  ferendum ;   nisi arte adiuventur. Nec ars alio modo possit adiuvare naturam nisi calore sive igne   Quod ut fiat, oportet ut pori corporum solutione aperiantur, quo natura operari possit   siquidem in congelato corpore metallico non appareant spiritus. Solutiones autem   sunt duplices vel violentae & inutiles, vel naturalis quae vera & unica est   quâ pori corporis aperiantur in aqua nostra quo digestum emittatur semen, &   matrici suae imponatur. Aqua vero nostra est caelestis non madefaciens  manus, non vulgi sed ferè pluvialis: Corpus est aurum quod semen dat: Luna   nostra est (non argentum vulgi) quae recipit semen auri. Regatur postea igne   continuo per menses 7 vel interdum 10, donec Aqua nostra consumat tres   & relinquat unum, & hoc in duplo. Postea nutritur lacte terrae vel  pinguedine ejus quae nascitur in uberibus Terrae, & regitur vel conservatur a  putrefactione Sale Naturae. Et sic generatur infans secundae generationis.   ‡ <in mg:>☞  ‡   Tractatus 11. Praxis   Lapidis . ℞  Terrae nostrae per 11 gradus 11 grana , auri   nostri granum 1, Lunae nostrae grana 2 (non Vulgi sed nostri vivi): postea pone in   ignem, & fiet inde liquor siccus; Terra primum resolvetur in aquam (quae dicitur ☿  Philosophorum) & illa aqua resolvet illa corpora ☉ is  & ☽ ae  &  consumet ea ut non maneat nisi pars decima cum una parte, & hoc erit humidum   radicale metallicum. Postea ℞  Aquam Salis Nitri de Terra nostra, in qua   est rivulus & unda viva si ad genuam foderis foveam, accipe igitur ex illa aquam   sed claram accipe, huic impone illud humidum radicale, & impone igni putrefactionis   & generationis, non tali ut in prima operatione fecisti: Rege cautè donec colores   appareant instar caudae Pavonis, rege digerendo, nec te taedeat donec cessent colores   & unus per totum appareat color viridis etc: & quando vides in fundo cineres   bruni coloris & aquam fere rubram, aperito vas madefacito pennam, inunge   ferrum aliquod ,si tingat infunde tantum aquae infra descriptae (quam in  promptu habebis ) quantum aeris intravit crudi & iterum coque ut prius donec   tingat iterum. Aqua vero illa debet esse menstruum mundi, ex sphaera   Lunae toties rectificatum quod possit calcinare Solem. Sic cuncta revelavi  praesertim in opere primo et secundo.   Ignis primus seu primae operationis est ignis unius gradus continuus qui   materiam circumdat: secundus est ignis naturalis qui materiam digerit & figit.   Vas Naturae esse debet & sufficiunt duo, in primo opere rotundum, in secundo vero   paulo minùs vitrum instar Phiolae vel ovi. Sed ignis naturae est unicus & effectuus   varietas tantum pendet a varietate locorum. Item Vas naturae est unicum sed nos   propter brevitatem binis utimur: materia una sed ex duabus substantiis. <4.3r> Page Image5    Nos nihil creamus (quod Dei est) sed res occultas & in umbra latentes Sublatis   umbris facimus apparere mediante Naturâ. Sic in aqua latent lapides salia aer terra   ignis etc in terra latent aqua ignis aer Sal etc in vapore metallico Metalla etc Et natura   facit ut haec aliquando appareant.   Solis caelestis calor temperatur ab aere terram cingente & Sol vel calor in centro   Terrae temperatur ab aqua poros terrae penetrante & refrigerat, alias omnia  consumerentur. Et iste calor centralis rarefacit & elevat aquam in aerem qui frigore in aquam   rursus densatur; aer qui spissus est ex sale vel pinguedine, distribuitur terrae unde varia nascuntur, tenuior   altius ascendit ad superficiem usque vel (si calore magno agitatur) in nubes antequam   densatur & hinc oriuntur Saxa Vegetabilia etc. Aer iste ab igne elementari repercussus   facit tonitrua etc . Sic aqua dicitur matrix aeris quia eum generat, & terra est   utriusque nutrix & receptaculum. ☞  Fac igitur ut talis sit operatio in nostra terrâ: ut calor centralis mutet   aquam in aerem, ut in planitiem mundi egrediatur & residuum per poros terrae   spargat, tunc in opposito aer vertetur in aquam multo subtiliorem quam fuit   prima: Et hoc fiet, si seni nostro aurum & argentum deglutire dabis ut   ipse consumat illa & tandem ille etiam moriturus comburatur, cineres ejus   spargantur in aquam coquito eam donec satis est & habes medicinam curandi   lepram. Cave ne frigidum pro calido vel calidum pro frigido sumas, naturam   misce naturae, & siquid est contrarium naturae separa (igne non manu) ut natura   sit naturae similis.   Tractatus 12 Elementa dependent ab invicem, unum ex alio vivit, & tamen   conjuncta non conveniunt; sed aqua est reliquis dignior quia mater omnium   rerum; hanc supernatat spiritus ignis. Causante igne aqua fit materia prima,   certatione videlicet ignis cum aquâ; & sic generantur venti vel vapores apti ut   congelentur cum terrâ , per aerem crudum qui ab initio separatus fuit ab illâ. Motus   causat calorem , calor movet aquam, aquae motus causat aerem omnium viventium   vitam. Crescunt igitur res ex aqua nam ex illius vapore subtiliori res leves &  subtiles proveniunt, ex oleo ejus graves & maioris pretii, ex sale autem res prioribus   longe pulchriores. Quia autem natura interdum impeditur ne res puras  producat (siquidem vapor, pinguedo, Sal maculantur, & miscent se terrae locis) ideo   nos docuit Natura purum ab impuro separare. Itaque solve corpus quodvis,   heterogeneum separa, purga, conjunge pura puris, cocta coctis, cruda crudis secundum   naturae pondus non materiae. Nam scito quod Sal Nitri centrale non accipit   plus de Terrâ nisi quantum opus habet, sive sit pura sive impura:   Aquae verò pinguedo aliter se habet nam nunquam habetur pura, ars   purgat illam per duplicem calorem & denuo conjungit illam.  Epilogus    Si nebula (i.e. corpus) obumbrans lumen naturae tollitur conspicias punctum magnetis   nostri correspondentis utrique centro radiorum Solis scilicet & lunae, Sic adolescens & puella 12   annorum similiter vestiti si iuncta locentur nemo distinguet quis sit mas quaeve femina   & sublatis vestibus statim distingues sexum. Sicut vestis est umbra vel tegumentum   corporis sic Corpus est umbra naturae vel seminis humanae   Creatus est homo de terrâ & ex aere vivit in quo est occultus vitae cibus, quem   nos rorem de nocte de die aquam vocamus rarefactam, cujus spiritus invisibilis   congelatus melior est quam Terra universa. <4.3v> Page Image6    ‡ <in mg:>☞  ‡   Praedicta repeto. ℞  aeris partes decem, auri vivi vel lunae vivae partem unam   haec omnia impone vasi & aerem coque imprimis ut sit aqua & postea non aqua.   Hic aer est veterum Philosophorum materia Id enim accipere debes quod est sed non   videtur donec artifici placeat; est aqua roris nostri ex qua extrahitur sal petrae   Philosophorum quo omnes res crescunt & nutriuntur : Matrix ejus est centrum Solis & ☽    tam caelestis quam terrestris: & magnes noster est quem prius Chalybem nominavi.   Aer generat Magnetem & Magnes generat & facit apparere aërem. Radii   Radiis junguntur in superficie Terrae & omnia producunt. Quando Pluvia fit, accipit   ex aere illam vim vitae & conjungit illam cum sale nitri Terrae (quod instar   Tartari calcinati aerem ad se trahit & in aquam solvit, istud (sal) aer fuit & est   conjunctus pinguedini Terrae) & quo radii solares copiosiùs  tunc  feriunt eo maior salis   nitri quantitas fit, unde et maior frumenti copia crescit, & hoc de die in diem   O caelum nostrum, aqua nostra, mercurius noster, Sal nitri nostrum in mari mundi versans, vegetabile   nostrum, 🜍  nostrum fixum,& volatile, caput mortuum seu faeces maris nostri, O aqua manus non   madefaciens manus sine qua nemo vivit nascitur generatur. Parabola seu Aenigma Philosophicum    Navigans austrum versus ad littus magni cuiusdam maris in incido mihi satis cogniti tamen nesciebam   an Piscis Echinis (ab omnibus solicitè quaesitus) ibi generaretur. Interea fessus dormio, & occurrit   venerandae canitiei senex Neptunus cum Tridente & ducit me in insulam versus Austrum   sitam rebus omnibus tum ad vitam necessariis tum ad delicias spectantibus refertissimam,   frugibus, herbis, floribus, plantis, etc. Item monstrabat sub aliqua rupe latentes duas illius insulae   mineras Auri & Chalybis. Non longe inde deducor ad pratum in quo Hortus variis   arboribus plantatus erat quarum septem erant nominibus insignitae & earum duae   reliquis eminebant, una ferens fructum instar solis lucidissimi & fulgentissimi, & folia <4.4r> Page Image7  ejus erat instar Auri. Altera vero producebat albissimos Lilliis candidiores fructus & 
  folia ejus erant sicut Argentum finum. Altera dicebatur arbor solaris altera   lunaris. Aqua tamen huic horto deerat quam multi frustra 4414r_1  tentabant haurire; & si  haurirent, tamen inutilis & venenosa foret nisi haurirent e radii 4424r_2  Solis vel Lunae. & talis   aquae (quae erat nivei candoris) nemo ultra decem partes potuit haurire. /  Neptuno  evanescente apparet mihi   vir magnus cujus fronti  Saturnus  erat inscriptus; & accepto vase hauriebat partes decem   & statim de fructu Arboris Solaris accepit & imposuit, qui statim resolvebatur &  consumebatur instar glaciei in aquâ calidâ. Dixi, video aquam esse fere ex nihilo & fructus   hos in illa adeo suavi calore consumi. Respondit, Haec est aqua vitae meliorans hos fructus   ut postea, non plantatione vel insitione, sed sola suo odore facit sibi caetera 6  arbores uniformes. Est instar faeminae huic fructui in qua putrescendo gignit Salamandram   in igne perseverantem, cujus Sanguis omni Thesauro est pretiosior, habens facultatem   Sex arbores has fertiles reddendi ut fructus melle dulciores proferant. Rogavi  quomodo istud? At ille; Vbi nunc unus saltem ex illo saturatur, quando in hac aquâ   coquitur possunt postea 1000 ex eo saturari. Rogo, coquiturne igne forti & quamdiu?   Ait, Aqua ista habet ignem internum & si iuvatur calore continuo comburit tres   partes corporis sui cum hoc corpore fructus ; & non remanebit nisi pars  minutissima quae vix imaginari potest sed in summa virtute, Coquitur 7 mensibus   primo deinde decem; Sed interea apparent res variae & semper in die quinquagesimo   plus vel minùs. Hoc Pomum in hac aqua decoctum (cui nihil aliud est addendum, ex quae 4434r_3    arbor solaris orta fuit , & quae extracta est ex radiis solis vel lunae vi magnetis)   fit fructus immortalis vitam habens & sanguinem qui sanguis omnes arbores steriles   facit portare fructus eiusdem naturae cum pomo. Haec aqua ubique est & miris   modis hauritur sed ista optima est quae extrahitur vi Calybis nostri qui invenitur   in ventre Arietis. Dixi ad quid utilis? ait, Ante debitam coctionem est summum   venenum sed coctione convenienti fit Summa medicina, & dat viginti novem   grana sanguinis; quodlibet autem granum tibi suppeditat 864 fructus Arboris   solaris. Et melioratur primo ad 10 deinde ad 100 tum ad 1000 etc ealtando. Istam   aquam omnes viderunt vident amant sed pauci noverunt, nomina habet varia   sed proprie dicitur Aqua maris nostri, Aqua vitae non madefaciens manus.   Omnes eâ utuntur sed invisibilitèr ex ea fiunt omnes & in eâ vivunt, sed in   eâ proprie nihil est, sed est res quae se miscet omni rei. Nec arboris fructus   melioratur sine hâc aquâ , nec est haec aqua utilis in hoc opere sine arboris   fructu. Et sic  Saturnus  evanuit. / Et  Neptunus  rursus apparuit quem rogavi, Domine   dicunt omnia generari ex mari & faeminâ, tamen  us  posuit solum fructum   arboris in ista aquâ at ille: Alio modo nascitur animal quadrupes alio vermis  vermes enim per putrefactionem oriuntur e terrâ quae ideo est eorum matrix & mater.   Sic aureus fructus putrefit in illa dictâ aquâ ejus matrice & matre) Et resurgit ex   cineribus Phoenix & Salamender Philosophorurm sic destructo corpore priori  corruptibili sese revivificat & resurgit immortalis. Sed si e conceptione duorum corporum fieret esset  mortalis siquidem mors nihil aliud est quam separatio unius ab alio. Rogavi, Domine   sunt ne diversae res rerum vel compositio in hoc opere? Ait, Unica res est cui   nil miscetur praeterquam aqua Philosophica tibi in Somno aliquoties manifestata   quae in decuplo esse debet ad unam corporis partem. Et crede haec non sunt somnia.   Mercurius ait. Certe fructus ab arbore non procul cadit, sed ego meam gloriam non   quaero: sum idem qui fui, sed anni mei diversi sunt. Ab initio Juvenis fui quamdiu   fui solus, sed jam senior sum sed idem qui fui ante. Frustra me visitas senem 
  qui me non nosti in juventute. Centrum meum est Cor omnium fixissimum  immortale & penetrans : in illo est requies Domini mei, ego vero ipse sum via &   cursor, peregrinus & domesticus, fidelissimus omnibus meis sociis, non relinquo  comitantes me; cum illis maneo cum illis pereo. Mater mea me genuit sed ego   sum senior matre, illa est Lapis Philosophorum: & frater meus qui habitat in arce in   Velle suo habet quod Philosophus vult. Alchemista Solviturné aurum in te? Mercurius   Quicquid mecum est amo sicut amicum & quicquid mecum nascitur huic   nutrimentum do ; & quicquid nudum est, alis meis tego. Alchemista Es ne tu magnus   Mercurius Ex. 1000 guttis unus ero, ex uno multa millia guttarum do: & sicut corpus   meum est in oculis tuis, si mecum ludere scis potes me dividere in tantum in  quantumvis, iterum unus ero quid igitur spiritus (cor meum) intrinsicè qui semper ex   minutissima parte multa millia profert? Ignis sum intus, ignis est mihi cibus   vita vero ignis aer est, sine aere ignis extinguitur; ignis praevalet aeri  propterea non quiesco, nec me crudus potest constringere aer; adde aerem aëri ut   sint ambo unum & teneant pondus, cum igne calido conjunge , temporibus   da ad custodiendum. Substrahetur Superfluum; residuum combures igne, in aquam   pones, coques, postea coctum aegrotis in medicinam dabis. Alchemista Quis est iste A 
 In Tractatu De Sulphure.    Circa fontem aquae plenam in Nemore deambulabant Sal & Sulphur altercantes   donec Sal intulit Sulphuri vulnus incurabile ex quo, loco sanguinis, aqua   quasi lac candidissimum effluxit ac in magnum flumen crevit. Et  Diana   nemore egressa in illa coepit lavari (i.e. in Fonte  Trevisano ) etc.   Alchemista sumebat partem cadaveris Sulphuris a Sale occisi, ei occurrit  Saturnus , tentarunt facere lapidem sine mercurio sed frustra. Tandem  Saturnus , accepit duo Argenta viva diversae substantiae sed unius radicis   & lavit urina sua & vocavit illa Sulphura de Sulphuribus, & commiscuit fixum cum volatili   & facta compositione in vas debitum posuit & ne 🜍  aufugeret custodens   supposuit, & sic postea in Balneum ignis lentissimum, ut materia  requirebat, posuit , & optime lapidem fecit.   A. Mercurius? Natura: Est mihi unus tantum talis filius unus ex septem est et  primus est, ipse quoque onmia est qui unus tantum erat nihil est et numerus ejus integer est   in illo sunt 4 Elementa qui tamen non est Elementum. Spiritus est qui tamen corpus habet. Vir est et tamen   vices feminae agit puer est & viri arma gerit. animal est & alas avis habet. Venenum est & lepram curat.   vita est & omnia occidet. Rex est, alter tamen possidet ejus regnum. fugit cum igne tamen ex illo puratur ignis   aqua est et non madefacit. terra est tamen seminatur.   Reductio lapidi exanimato animam restituit & rorido ac spirituali lacte  nutrit dum sit perfectus [in hisce duobus ultimus Draco caudam suam devorando se 
  exhaurit] (4) fixatio utriusque 🜍 is  supra suum corpus mediante spiritu  tincturarum. Mediatore et Elixir perficit.  64 3  Media demonstrativa Colores (1) niger initium actionis 
  ignis nox nigerrimus perfecta solutio & confusio Elementorum. (2) albus perfectio 
🜍 is  albi lapidis benedicti & terrae albae foliatae in qua 
  Philosophi aurum suum seminant. (3) Citrinus. 4 Rubeus sive sanguineus, 
🜍  rubrum. Sperma masculinum, Ignis Lapidis, Corona regia filius ☉ is  primi 
  labora finis.  66 Nigredo repetita mala est corvorum pulli ad nidum non  regrediantur. Praecox rubedo ariditatis argumentum est & solo caelo  imbrem illico largiente avertitur rubedo semel apparet fine. 
 67 Quatuor digestiones lapidis quibus quasi per gradus exaltatur & perficitur 
  supradictis 4 Regimenibus correspondent  68 (1) solutio, utriusque seminis commixtio, putrefactio 
  resolutio Elementorum in aquam homogeneam Eclipsis ☉ is  in capite draconis,  69 (2) Spiritus Dei ambulat 
  super aquas, lux incipit, aquae separantur ab aquis, Corvorum pulli in  Columbas  mutatis  pennis migrare incipiunt, Aqua Philosophorum ignis est quae (calore balnei excitante) sursum & 
  deorsum movetur sed cave ne terra exsiccetur aut deundetur.  70 (3) Tertia digestio Terrae 
  recens natae lac roridum ac omnes spirituales quintae essentiae virtutes propinat animamque 
  vivificam spiritu mediante corpori alligat tunc Terra fit corruscanti ☽ ae  primo deinde ☉ i   ruberrimo similis. neutra paenas ignis amplius timet quia carent omni labe  71 (4) per quartam Terra in fermentum praestantissimum conversa 
<5.3r> Page Image5  omnia imperfecta corpora fermentat. Hoc naturae & artis miraculum furnus 
  secretus Philosophorum repetitis primi operis digestionibus tibi pandet. 
 72 Totum opus est solutio & congelatio. Per Reductionem corpus volatile figitur 
  quemadmodum fixa prius in volatilem transierat. Quamdiu erant confusae naturae 
  spiritus ille mixtus mediam sustinet naturam inter volatile & fixum. 
 74. Ut Eva data est Adae: sic per rorem Caeli datur lapidi virtus multiplicandi 
  mediante copula utriusque sexûs etc. 
 78 Aquae per septem gyros seu revolutiones circumactae (donec ex sole  generatur saturnus) succedit aer qui totidem circulis & reductionibus rotatur   donec figatur deorsum &  o  expulso ♃  regnat cujus adventu Infans Phil:  formatur nutretur ac tandem in lucem prodit facie splendorem ☽ ae  referente. Ignis 
  naturae demum, externo igne urgente, ex occulto fit manifestus: Lilium tunc 
  tingit crocus: candentis pueri genas rubor occupat & Regi futuro corona paratur. 
  Sic tandem in terrâ omnia elementa quiescunt (incipientes ab aqua) & opus primum perficitur 
 83 Circulatio Elementorum exercetur vel per Rotam majorem (quae omnia 
  Elementa figit in Terra, eiusque circulus cum opere Sulphuris absoluto 
  finitur) vel per Rotam minorem cujus revolutio in extractione &  praeparatione cuiusque elementi terminatur, huic autem Rotae 3 insunt circuli 
  qui materiam jugiter & varie agitant ac pluries, septies ad minus unum 
  quodque Elementum circumagunt sibi invicem & ordinatè succedentes & ita 
  consentanei ut si unus defecerit caeterorum labor irritus fiat. Primus 
  movet sursum per calorem desiccando alter deorsum per frigus humectando, tertius vero 
  quietis & somni imaginem referens digerendo in summâ temperie utriusque 
  cessationem inducit./ Primus est evacuatio et subtrahit humidum superfluum 
  & separat purum mundum & subtile a faecibus crassis. Summum periculum 
  in hoc circulo versatur quia spiritualia tractat, & naturam exuberat. Cave 
  itaque ne motu nimis intenso & accelerato confundas subtile & crassum (spiritus 
  enim mixtione terrae corruptus fit hebes & invalidus) item nè motu prolixiori 
  & nimis longo terra spiritu suo nimis evacuatur, et arida & languens spiritu 
  destituta reddatur. Uterque error tincturas comburet vel etiam in fugam 
  vertit. / Secundus Circulus est Restauratio, anhelanti & debilitato corpori 
  potu vires restituit. Prior circulus sudoris & laboris fuit organum hic  refrigerii & consolationis. Hac in trituranda & emollienda Terra figulorum 
  more versatur quò melius misceatur. Huius motus sit prioris motu 
  levior maximè in principio revolutionis, ne corvorum pulli in nido 
  suo largo flumine submergantur & mundus nascens diluvio deleatur,   Itaque leges hujus circuli sunt ut lente & paulatim currat & pares effundat ne ignis insitus  extinguatur. Item alternis vicibus cibus & potus administretur ut melior fiat digestio 
  & optimum sicci & humidi temperamentum. Tantum irrigando adjicias 
  quantum assando defecerit. Restauratio corroborando deperditarum virium 
  tantum restituat quantum evacuatio abstulerit debilitando. / Tertius circulus 
  est digestio, tacito & insensibili motu agens (quare in furno secreto fieri dicitur) 
  Putrefactio etiam dicitur qui 5313r_1  alimentum calore quasi stommochali quodamodo putrefacit 
  ut meliùs figatur & ex mercuriali in sulphuream naturam commutetur. 
  Inhumatio etiam dicitur quia spiritus quasi mortuus per eum humo sepelitur 
  Duo priores circuli in solvendo praesertim laborans hic in congelando licèt 
  omnes utrumque operentur. Huius circuli leges sunt ut febrili & lentissimi 
<5.3v> Page Image6  fimi calore moveatur; ne volatilia effugiant & turbetur spiritus cum 
  tempore strictissimae ejus conjunctionis cum corpore. Cave ne ullis ventis 
  aut imbribus commoveatur terra. Sic per opera interrupta & per vices tres 
  illi circuli erratici (quorum secundus semper debet succedere primo & tertius 
  secundo) unam circulationem integram complent quae pluries repetita omnia 
  tandem in terram figit. 
 93. Nomen Ignis quandoque pro calore sumitur & tum sunt tot ignes quot 
  calores. In generatione mineralium & metallorum est triplex caelestis a Sole 
  terrestris in visceribus terrae vapores excitans etc & insitus qui a solar calore 
  genitus intus in metallis quasi incarceratus retinetur & semini insitus materiam disponit 
  & informat, quem calor ☉ is  externus a torpedine excitat etc 
  94 In Lapide faciendo duplex est ignis insitus vel externus; insitus est triplex 
  (1) Naturalis qui materiae strictissimè devinctus & insitus hebes stupescit donec externo 
  calore liber factus explicando penetrando & congelando tandem humidam suam 
  materiam informat. (2) Innaturalis qui accitus & extrinsecus adveniens per 
  successivos digestionis gradus in materiam inductus est et in ignem naturalem 
  convertitur. Sic ignis naturalis & insitus qui simplex sibi tantum sufficit 
  nec ultra habet quod eroget in corporibus aliis tingendis augetur &  multiplicatur ut per exuberatam tincturam & abstrusum ignis   multiplicati Thesaurum aliena & imperfecta corpora tingat. Et totum 
  arcanum in hujus ignis multiplicatione consistit. (3) Ignis Contra naturam 
  est qui compositum putrefacit imperfectus est quia non valet ad  generationem. Et talis est ignis seu calor menstrui. Revera non differt ab igne 
  insito sed est prior & debilior ejus gradus ad corpus emolliendum & 
  praeparandum, ut a succendente calore urgentiore ad generationem promoveatur. 
  Et hunc duplicem caloris gradum duplex sequitur motus, lentior &  vegetior.  97. Aquam etiam suum 5313v_1  ignem vel aquam ignis vocant Philosophi, quia igneo spiritu 
  imbuta est; dicitur et aqua ardens quia perfecta metalla dissolvit 
  cremat comburit quae igni resistant. Ignis in ventre Aquae occultus per 
  duplicem solutionis & multiplicationis effectum se prodit. 
 98 Ignis externus insitum evocat & regit, itaque ejus gradus & puncta 
  disce aut opus hoc Philosophicum ne aggredere quia semper per gradus & puncta minima debet paulatim movere. Gradus 
  sunt Balnei Cinerum Carbonum & Flammae (sive opteticus). Ignis  carbonum & Flammae promiscue dicitur ardens. Calorum puncta sunt 
  calor febrilis seu fimi, Balnei simpliciter, Cinerum simpliciter, arenae 
  remissus & intensus calor Carbonum & Flammae. 
 106 Totum opus consistit in seperatione & preparatione Elementorum 
  quorum quodvis proprio ignis gradu extrahitur. Tria nempe in opere 
  albo cum tribus tantum ignis gradibus & unum in rubro: Per primum gradum fit Eclipsis 
☉ is  & ☽ ae , per secundum ☽ ae  lumen incipit restaurari, per tertium ☽ a  fit plena 
  per quartum opteticum ☉  exaltatur. Veteres ut occultarent ignis gradum varium 
  nominarunt aereum vaporosum humidum siccum clarum astralem etc: Plura 
  de ignis notitia desideranti  Lullius  satisfaciet. 
 109 Vas vel est naturae (terra lapidis, Femella, matrix recipiens masculi  seminas) vel artis & est triplex Ovum Philosophicum [Cuius formam ut occultarent 
  dixerunt componi ex variis Alembico, Cucurbita, coopertorio etc nominarunt vas 
<5.4r> Page Image7  solutorium, putrefactorium, distillatorium, sublimatorium calcinatorium etc pro varietate 
  operationum; sed unicum vas artis tantùm ad utriusque Sulphuris opus terminandum  sufficit & opus Elixiris aliud. Nec ad primi operis finem vas aperiatur] Vas dicitur caecum vel cucurbita caeca. quia 
  occulus occaecatur sigillo Hermetico; ejus collum unius palmi aut ultra prominens, satis  amplum ore stricto ad modum ampullae factum & undique spissum quo igni longo 
  quandoque & acuto resistat. Secundum vas ligneum ex trunco quercino in duo 
  hemisphaeria concava secto contineat primum: vide fontem  Trevisani . Vas tertium est 
  Furnus reliqua continens ex cocto latere vel terra pingui vel argilla optimè 
  tritâ & cum fimo equino pilis admixtis praeparatâ ne longo calore rimosa fiat. 
  Spissi fiant parietas 3 aut 4 digitorum quo calorem sistere eique validus resistere 
  possit. Sit. Formâ rotundâ, altitudo interior duorum circiter pedum in cujus 
  medio statuatur lamina ferrea aut aenea circularis dorsi cultelli crassitudinem 
  habens furno interiore paulo angustior ne parietes tangat, 3 aut 4 fulcris 
  parietibus affixis innixa & crebris foraminibus pervia, ut calor per ea & 
  oras: laminae faciliùs ascendat & Regatur. Infra Laminam sit ostiolum 
  pro igne supponendo & aliud supra pro calore sentiendo: ad cujus oppositam 
  partem fiat fenestella figurae romboides vitro munita ut lux adversa 
  oculo admoto colores 5414r_1  indicet. Laminae supradictae umbilico tripes arcanorum 
  cum duplici vase imponatur. Denique tecto continuo & cohaerente optime 
  opperiatur ostiolis stricte clausis ne calor exhalet. 
 119 Aquilae quo  pluris  a 3 ad 10 eo facilius leonem vincunt faelicior 
  numerus 7 ex  Lullio  vel 9 ex  Seniore . Ut utrumque 🜍  componas ℞   Draconem ruffum animosum bellicosum cui natalis roboris nil desit. & 7 aut 9  Aquilas virgines quarum acies oculorum ad Solem non hebescit, Balneo supposito 
  incita ad pugnam circa diem aves feriam dilaniare incipient illa moriens 
  tabo & nigro veneno totum carcerem inficiet quo aquilae infectae etiam moriuntur 
  ex putrefactione cadaverum generatur Corvus qui paulatim emergente capite  baleoque aliquantum aucto volare incipit, diu circum volans, et lenta longaque pluvia 
  ac rore caelesti dealbatus in Cygnum candidissimum mutatur. In corvo dealbando  extrahe Elementa & ex ordine praescripto distilla, donec in terra sua figatur & in 
  niveos pulveres desinat. / Igne sensim roborato urge materiam donec occultum 
  incipit patefieri cujus index sit emergens color citrinus. Ignem quarti gradus 
  per sua puncta rege donec ex lilio fiant rosae purpureae tandemque  amaranthus fusco sanguinis rubore tinctus. Sed ignem igne evocabis donec in  cineris rubicundissimo & tactui in sensibiles convertitur. 
 123. Ex qua materiâ gignitur hoc sulphur primum (dictum Lapis ignis, pater & semen 
  masculinum metallorum ea apperiens & penetrans etc) ex eadem multiplicatur 
  addita (& fit secandum) portiunculâ primi. (Consule  Lulliam ) sed debet cum perfectis metallis 
  fermentari, priusquam projectio fieri possit, ut inde perfectum habeas 
  Elixir, quod ex triplici, componitur materia, Aqua Metallicâ sive ☿ o  ut priùs sublimato 
  Fermento albo vel rubro (id est argento vel auro puro pro intentione operantis) & secundo illo Sulphure. Extra 
  perfecta metalla ☉ em  & ☽  non invenitur perfecta tinctura , quâ 🜍  tuum fermentari 
  possit, Item cave ut ☉  & ☽  sunt pura & distincta Fermenta ne commisceas enim 
  duo luminaria (Elixira) sua sulphura diversa & tincturas distinctas requirunt.  Secundum opus in eodem vase aut simili, eodem furno ac iisdem ignis gradibus ac 
  primum coquitur sed breviore tempore perficitur. ℞  Terrae rubeae sive Fermenti 
  rubei tria pondera. Aquae & Aeris una pondus duplum simul optime trita misceantur 
  fiat Amalgama instar butyri vel pastae metallinae ita ut terra emollita tactui 
  sit insensibilis adde ignis unum pondus cum dimidio, in vase sigillata igne primi 
<5.4v> Page Image8  gradus quantum sufficiat denigrantur, deinde suis ignis gradibus extrahantur 
  Elementa ex ordine donec paulatim in terra sua figantur ut materia tandem 
  diu rupem illuminatam & diaphanam terminabitur. Cuius partem pro voto 
  accipe & in Crucibulum projectam igne levi guttatim oleo suo rubeo pota 
  & incera quousque omnino fundatur & fluat sine fumo. Nex fugam 
  time potûs enim suavitate Terra emollita eum intra sua viscera susceptum 
  sistet: Et Elixir Completum habes. Pro Elixare albo idem est ordo ac methodus 
  modo albis tantum elementis ejus compositione utaris: Et opus in laminam 
  candidam splendentem & quasi christallinam desinet quae oleo suo albo 
  incerata fusionis gradum adipiscetur. De alterutro Elixare proiice 1 supra 
  10 ☿ ii  abluti & effectum attonitus miraberis. 
 125. Elixar perfectum tincturam & fixationem habet a Fermento, penetrationem 
  a Sulphure, fusionem ab argento vivo (quod medium est conjugendi tincturas   fermenti nempe & 🜍 is ) & multiplicativam virtutem a spiritu quintae  essentiae infusam mutuatur.  129 Tres sunt in Lapide humores qui successivè sunt extrahendi aqueus,  aëreus & radicalis (sive ignis qui omnium tenacissimus quia Naturae centro 
  alligatus est a quo non facilè separatur) hos tres sensim & successive 
  extrahe per rotas suas solvendo & congelando donec rota extensa totumque opus 
  absolvitur. 
 133. Prava corporum accidentia quae puritatem eorum & verum Naturae 
  lumen tenebris obsidebant per longas & multiplices sublimationes ac  digestiones deleta sunt. Et ignis naturae vires per inspiratum quintae  essentiae spiritum uberrimè multiplicatae sunt & virtutem adhuc multiplicativam 
  inde acquisiverunt quod fit tripliciter. Primo, Elixiris Rubei pondus 
  unum aquae suae rubeae ponderibus 9 admisce, in vase solutorio in aquam 
  dissolve, materiam optime dissolutam ac unitam lento igne decoquendo  coagula, donec in Rubinum sive laminam rubeam roboretur, quam postea oleo 
  suo rubeo incera donec fluat; & medicina erit decuplo potentior: via brevi 
  & facilis. Secundus modus est: Elixaris  tui  proportionem ad libitum cum aquâ 
  suâ mixtam, servatis ponderibus in vase Reductionis optimè sigilla per  inhumationem in Balneo dissolve, dissolutam distilla, Elementa successivè   separando propriis ignibus, ac deorsum figendo ut in primo & secundo opere factum 
  est donec lapidescat; incera, projice. Haec via longior et locupletior ad  centuplum auget virtutem; nam quo subtilior fit per reiteratas operationes 
  eo plus virium superorum & inferorum  retinet . Tertius modus: Elixaris ita 
  multiplicati proijce 1 supra 100 ☿ ii  abluti & modico tempore ☿ us  inter primus 
  calefactus in purum Elixir convertetur, cujus 1 supra 100 similis 7 ☿ ii  erit ☉ .
